- To stop a run that costs too much, set Stop when to a Time cap.

### 19.5 Frames monitor

**Product:** Actuation · **Kind:** effect · **Pane:** a pane in Factory; also the Frames widget

You need to see exactly what passed between the shell and the harness. The Frames monitor shows every frame the gateway sends or receives, per session, in order. It works like the MIDI monitor in Live.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Session | All sessions · The lead · One session | All sessions | Shows the frames of one session, or of all. |
| Direction | Both · Sent · Received | Both | Shows frames the shell sent, frames it received, or both. |
| Kinds | Messages · Tools · Heartbeat · Approvals · Errors | All | The kinds of frame to show. |
| Follow | On · Off | On | Scrolls with new frames. |
| Pause | On · Off | Off | Holds the list still. The gateway keeps the frames. |
| Keep | 100–5000 frames | 1000 | The number of frames in the list. |

#### Views

```ui
device: actuation:frames-monitor
caption: Frames monitor, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: in a pane of Factory. The folded monitor shows the title "Frames" and the frame rate. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: in a pane of Factory. The compact monitor shows the last four frames of the selected session. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Session | Chooses All sessions, the lead or one session. |
| Kinds | Chooses Messages, Tools, Heartbeat, Approvals or Errors. |
| Direction | Chooses Both, Sent or Received. |
| Pause | Holds the list still. The gateway keeps the frames. |

Click the plate to show the monitor whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open the full frame list in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole monitor shows every parameter, in two groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Filter | Session, Direction, Kinds |
| Display | Follow, Pause, Keep |

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full monitor is the frame list. Each frame shows its session, its direction and its time. Beside the list, four readings show: Readiness, Liveness, Replay and Upstream. Select a frame to see its task, turn and step. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the last eleven frames of the selected session, newest at the bottom. A frame is an event such as tool.start or approval.request. The widget only reads. It sends no frame. Click the open mark to open the monitor in a pane. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on the Frames monitor |
|---|---|
| Follow | Keeps the list on the selected session. |
| Position | Shows the task, turn and step of the frame you select. |
| Tap | Adds a status frame from every running agent. |

#### To use the Frames monitor
1. Select the session whose lane stalled.
2. Open the Frames monitor from the Gateway, or from the Frames widget.
3. Set Session to that session. Set Kinds to Tools and Errors.
4. Read the last frames. They show what the lane did last.
5. Select a frame. The Position field shows its task, turn and step.

#### Rules
- The monitor only reads. It sends nothing and changes no frame.
- The Replay reading shows the events in the ring and its size. A new epoch resets the cursor.
- A frame carries its session, its direction and its time. The list keeps this order.

#### Tips
- To find the tool that stalled a session, filter to Tools and read the last frame.
- To check that a note reached the agent, filter to Messages and look for the steer line.

### 19.6 Skillset rack

**Product:** AIKit · **Kind:** rack · **Chain:** third device on a Factory session's chain

An agent works from what it carries: what it can do, how it acts, and how it
finds its bearings in a field. The Skillset rack holds that repertoire. Use it
to see what the agent carries, what is really available to it now, and what it
has used.

#### Four kinds of carried practice

| Kind | It answers | What it is | Its mark: the blade · the pen |
|---|---|---|---|
| Skill | What can I do? | One capability. | One blade · one pen |
| Method | How do I act? | How one piece of work goes from claim to return: the skills, sources and checks it composes. A step that the work does not touch is skipped. | The forge · the compass and the set square |
| Methodology | How do I orient? | The whole practice of a field: which kinds of work exist there, which Method applies when, and where the return goes. It is loaded before work in its field. | The book with the sword in the stone · the drawing board |
| Skillset | What do I carry? | A repertoire of skills, methods, methodologies and other sets. | Blades in a rack · instruments in a pot |

A Method and a Methodology are skills whose text states their form. AIKit
trusts, enables and delivers them as it does every skill. Choose the marks in
Settings: the blade or the pen. The whole shell uses the one set.

A mark has three states, and the drawing does not change between them. A full
line: AIKit makes the member available. A line in orange: AIKit holds the
member back for a reason that you can clear, such as a revision that nobody
has reviewed. A dim line: the member is withheld, because you turned it off,
its author retired it, or a policy refuses it.

#### What a member has done

Each member shows ten steps of evidence, from carried to verified. A step is
lit only when there is evidence for it. A step that nobody observed stays
hollow. Nothing is inferred upward.

| Step | It is lit when |
|---|---|
| Carried | The agent's sets hold the member. |
| Catalogued | AIKit finds the member in its catalogue. |
| Available | The member passes its trust and policy checks here. |
| Selected | The member is chosen for the act in hand. |
| Projected | The member is written into the harness for this context. |
| Loaded | The member's text entered the agent's context. |
| Invoked | The agent called the member in this session. |
| Relied on | The return names the member as material to the result. |
| Succeeded | The act that it served produced its expected return. |
| Verified | An independent check passed. |

Carried is not loaded, and loaded is not used. A set holds a member. It does
not make the agent use it.

#### Parameters

| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Set | A set, by its name | The set on the chain | Chooses the repertoire. Drag a set from the browser to replace it. |
| Member | On · Off, one for each member | On | Your request to use the member, through the profile in use. On grants no trust and loads nothing. |
| Scope | Home · Project · Session | Project | Where the composition applies: every session on this machine, every session in this project, or this session only. |
| Profile | A governance profile | Baseline | The profile that the composition applies under. |
| Apply | Preview, then Apply | — | Shows what a change would apply, then applies it. A new change cancels the preview. |
| Package target | Claude · Codex · Pi · OpenAI | Claude | The harness to package the set for. |
| Export | An action | — | Writes the native package and its receipt. The set does not change. |
| Mark state | Available · Held · Withheld (reading) | Read from AIKit | The state of each member, with the reason. |
| Evidence | Ten steps for each member (reading) | Read from AIKit | What each member has done in this session. |
| Revision | A digest (reading) | Read from AIKit | The revision of the whole set. |
| Unsupported | A count (reading) | Read from the receipt | The relations that the package target cannot carry. The receipt names each one. |

#### Views

```ui
device: ai-kit:skillset-rack
width: 1500
height: 1000
caption: The Skillset rack on Central's documentation set. Turn a member off and the Preview lights. Point at a mark or at the ticks to read them. Switch the fixture to see the pen marks.
```

##### Folded

Where: on a Factory session's chain, as the third device. The folded rack shows
the set's name and how many members are loaded. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: on a Factory session's chain. The compact rack shows its members as a
chain list: your request as a square, the member's mark in its state, its name
and its ten steps of evidence. Methodologies come first, then methods, skills
and child sets. A long list pages.

| Control | What it does |
|---|---|
| Scope | Sets Home, Project or Session. |
| Preview, Apply | Shows the change, then applies it. |
| Loaded | Shows how many members are loaded, of all that the set carries. |

Click a square to turn a member on or off. Click the plate to show the rack
whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open the set full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole rack has two bands.

| Band | Sections |
|---|---|
| One | Set: the set, its members by kind, the revision, the author. Compose: Scope, Preview and Apply, Profile. Package: the target, Export, the unsupported count. |
| Two | Every member, in two columns, with its square, mark, evidence and revision. |

Select a member to show it whole in the pool (see §19.7).

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full rack is the set as a page, grouped by kind:
methodologies, methods, skills and sets. Each member shows its mark, its
revision, its evidence, its state and what it is for. The controls stay in
Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the four marks with a count
under each, and three counts: available, loaded and invoked. The widget stays
when you change mode.

#### Transport

| Control | Effect on the Skillset rack |
|---|---|
| Keys and maps | A key or a spoken word can turn a member on or off. |
| Capture | Turns a task you did by hand into a proposal for a new method. See Capture skill. |

#### To use the Skillset rack

1. Drag a set from the browser onto the chain of a session.
2. Read the marks. Point at a dim mark to read why it is withheld.
3. Select a member with an orange mark. Review its revision to make it
   available.
4. Click a member's square to turn it on or off. Click Preview, read the
   change, then click Apply.
5. Read the evidence. A member that is carried but never loaded costs the
   agent nothing. A member that is loaded but never invoked costs it context.
6. To give the set to another harness, choose the target and click Export.

#### Rules

- Membership grants no trust, no authority and no loading.
- The marks and the evidence are readings. The shell does not light them.
- A set carries a child set by reference. A change to the child reaches every
  set that carries it.
- A change applies only after Preview and Apply.

#### Tips

- To keep an agent's context light, turn off members that it loads and never
  invokes.
- To share a set with a team on another harness, read the unsupported count
  before you export it.

### 19.7 Skill, Method, Methodology

**Product:** AIKit · **Kind:** effect · **Chain:** one chain inside a Skillset rack

You need to see one member of a repertoire closely: what it is for, whether
the agent can use it now, and what it did. One device shows all three forms,
and its face changes with the form.

#### Three faces

| Form | The face shows | You can |
|---|---|---|
| Skill | Its mark, its evidence, the harnesses it reaches and the sets that carry it. | Turn it on or off. Review its revision. |
| Method | Its mark, its evidence, and the path that it composes, from claim to return, with the members that each step uses. | Run it once, after a preflight. Prove the last run. |
| Methodology | Its mark, its evidence, its field, and which Method applies when. | Read whether the act in hand needs it. |

#### Parameters

| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Member | A member of the rack | — | Which member the chain holds. |
| Enabled | On · Off | On | Your request, through the profile in use. |
| Trust | Trusted · Unseen (reading) | Read from AIKit | AIKit does not deliver an unseen revision until you review it. |
| Review | An action | — | Records that you reviewed the revision. You confirm it first. |
| Revision | A number (reading) | Read from AIKit | The revision in use. |
| Evidence | Ten steps (reading) | Read from AIKit | What the member has done in this session. See §19.6. |
| Run | An action, Method only | — | Runs the Method once and keeps a receipt. A preflight checks first: a missing condition refuses the run before any effect and names itself. |
| Prove | An action, Method only | — | Checks the receipt of the last run. A run that did not verify is refused. |
| Instantiate | Needed · Not needed (reading), Methodology only | Read for each act | Whether the act in hand needs the Methodology, or the invoked skill carries enough. |
| Reach | Harnesses (reading), Skill only | Read from AIKit | The harnesses that the skill is written into in this context. |

#### Views

```ui
device: ai-kit:skill-device
width: 1500
height: 1000
caption: One member device, three forms. Switch the fixture: a Method and its path, a Methodology and its table, a Skill, and a Method whose revision nobody has reviewed.
```

##### Folded

Where: on one chain inside a Skillset rack. The folded member shows its name,
its form and its revision. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: on one chain inside a Skillset rack. The compact member shows its mark
large, its form and the question the form answers, and its evidence.

| Control | What it does |
|---|---|
| Enabled | Your request to use it. |
| Trust or Review | Shows Trusted, or offers Review for an unseen revision. |
| Run (Method) | Runs the Method after a preflight. |
| Instantiate (Methodology) · Reach (Skill) | Shows the reading for the form. |

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. Band one holds the mark with the ten steps
named, then Standing (Enabled, Trust, Revision), then the form's own section:
Run for a Method, Orients for a Methodology, Reach for a Skill. Band two holds
the form's body: the path that a Method composes, the table of which Method
applies when for a Methodology, or what a Skill is for.

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full member is its text: its form, what it says, a Method's
steps, a Methodology's table. Its evidence and state stand under the text. The
controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the mark, the evidence, and Run
for a Method. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport

| Control | Effect on the member |
|---|---|
| Play | Runs a selected Method, after its preflight. |
| Keys and maps | A key or a spoken word can turn the member on or off. |

#### To use the member device

1. Select a member in the Skillset rack. It shows whole in the pool.
2. Read its mark and its evidence.
3. If the mark is orange, read the reason. Click Review, read the revision,
   then confirm.
4. For a Method, click Run. Read the preflight. Confirm to run it.
5. After a run, click Prove to check the receipt.

#### Rules

- The form comes from the member's own text. To change the form, change the
  text.
- A Method composes. It does not order its steps, and it skips a step that the
  work does not touch.
- A Methodology points at its Methods. It does not hold their text.
- A change to a member makes a new revision. The old revision stays. A new
  revision is unseen until you review it.

#### Tips

- To learn a field quickly, open its Methodology full in a pane and read its
  table of Methods.
- To find a Method that nobody runs, read the evidence: carried, never
  selected.

### 19.8 Knowledge route

**Product:** AIKit · **Kind:** effect · **Chain:** inside the Scope device, in a Factory session's chain

You need to choose which knowledge an agent can look up, and how far it may follow links. The Knowledge route sets the sources and the bounds. The agent then finds what it needs on demand.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sources | Wiki · Project knowledge · Code · Web | Wiki, Project knowledge | The places the agent may search. |
| Reach | Object · Scene · Expression · Central | As Scope | The widest part of the world whose knowledge the agent may read. The Scope device holds the same value. |
| Search first | On · Off | On | The agent searches before it reads a file. |
| Link depth | 0–4 | 2 | The number of links the agent follows from a hit. |
| Read budget | 1–200 pages | 40 | The most pages the agent reads in one task. |
| Route | Ready · Composed · Refused (reading) | Read from AIKit | Shows whether the route answers. |

#### Views

```ui
device: ai-kit:knowledge-route
caption: Knowledge route, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: inside the Scope device, in a Factory session's chain. The folded route shows the title "Knowledge" and the count of sources. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: inside the Scope device. The compact route shows a small graph of the sources in reach. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Sources | Chooses Wiki, Project knowledge, Code or Web. |
| Reach | Sets the widest part of the world whose knowledge the agent may read. |
| Link depth | Sets the number of links the agent follows from a hit. |
| Read budget | Sets the most pages the agent reads in one task. |

Click the Scope title to open its reads and writes whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open the knowledge full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole route shows every parameter, in two groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Sources | Sources, Reach, Route |
| Bounds | Search first, Link depth, Read budget |

The route cannot reach farther than Scope allows.

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full route shows the knowledge in reach, as a list and as a graph. A page that the agent read shows with its source. Open the source from the page. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the pages that the agent read in this task, with their sources. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on the Knowledge route |
|---|---|
| Key, root and scale | Shows tokens in and out, which includes the pages the agent read. |
| Draw | Points the agent at a page or a node. |
| Follow | Keeps the Graph and Wiki widgets on what the agent reads. |

#### To use the Knowledge route
1. Select a session. Open Device view.
2. Open the Scope device whole in the pool.
3. Choose the Sources.
4. Set the Reach. Use a small Reach for a narrow task.
5. Set Link depth. Use 0 when the agent must stay on the page you pointed at.

#### Rules
- Sources are not copied into the prompt. The agent finds them and reads them on demand.
- The route cannot reach farther than Scope allows.
- A page that the agent read is shown with its source. You can open the source.
- A route that is Refused names the reason. The agent then works without that source.

#### Tips
- To keep the agent on topic, lower Link depth.
- To let an agent check the wiki first, keep Search first on.

### 19.9 Agency gateway

**Product:** AIKit · **Kind:** effect · **Chain:** one chain in the Gateways rack

You need messages to reach an agent from any surface you use, and from one agent to another, under rules you set. The Agency gateway carries those messages. It is a service that keeps running while you work.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Service | On · Off | On | Starts or stops the gateway. |
| Surfaces | A list of surfaces | The ones you use | The places that may send a message to an agent: this window, a terminal, a chat. |
| Agent to agent | Allow · Ask · Deny | Ask | Whether one agent may call another. Ask puts the call in Approvals. |
| Harnesses | A list (reading) | Found on this machine | The harnesses that the gateway can reach. |
| Inbox | A count (reading) | Read from the gateway | The messages that wait for an agent. |
| State | Ready · Composed · Refused (reading) | Read from the gateway | Whether the gateway carries messages now. |

#### Views

```ui
device: ai-kit:agency-gateway
caption: Agency gateway, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: as one chain in the Gateways rack, on the Main track. The folded gateway shows the title "Agency gateway" and its state dot. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: as one chain in the Gateways rack. The compact gateway shows the surfaces on the left, the agents on the right, and the messages between them. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Service | Starts or stops the gateway. |
| Surfaces | Chooses the places that may send a message to an agent. |
| Agent to agent | Sets Allow, Ask or Deny for a call from one agent to another. |
| State | Shows Ready, Composed or Refused. |

Click the row to read its state. Click the plate to show the gateway whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open it full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole gateway shows every parameter, in three groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Service | Service |
| Rules | Surfaces, Agent to agent |
| Readings | Harnesses, Inbox, State |

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full gateway shows the messages that passed. It also lists the refusals with their reasons. A refused call is also an event in the activity record. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the count of messages that wait, and the state dot. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on the Agency gateway |
|---|---|
| Link | Adds the gateways of your other machines. |
| Tap | Sends a status request through the gateway to every running agent. |

#### To use the Agency gateway
1. Select the Main track. Open Device view.
2. Click the Agency gateway row in the Gateways rack.
3. Check that Service is On and the state is Ready.
4. Choose which Surfaces may send messages.
5. Set Agent to agent to Ask until you trust the agents.

#### Rules
- A refused call becomes an event in the activity record. It does not vanish.
- The gateway carries a message. It does not act for the agent.
- An agent that you did not seat receives no message.

#### Tips
- To let a planner agent hand work to a builder, set Agent to agent to Allow for that pair.
- To keep a session private, turn off all Surfaces except this window.

### 19.10 Capture skill

**Product:** AIKit · **Kind:** instrument · **Chain:** on the armed Factory track

You need an agent to learn something you did by hand. Capture skill turns a worked example into a skill. You do the task once. The device writes a skill from it and sends the skill to you as a proposal.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source | The last minutes · A selected clip | The last 10 minutes | The part of your work that the skill learns from. |
| Name | Text | A name from the example | The skill's name. |
| Fit | Exact · Light · Broad | Light | How far the skill generalises. Exact repeats your steps. Broad adapts them to new cases. |
| Scope | This project · All my work | This project | Where the skill may be used. |
| Steps | A list (reading) | Read from the example | The steps the device found. You can remove a step. |
| Lands as | Proposal | Proposal | Where the skill goes. It goes to Approvals. |

#### Views

```ui
device: ai-kit:capture-skill
caption: Capture skill, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: on the armed Factory track's chain. The folded device shows the title "Capture skill" and a dot. The dot is red while it records. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: on the armed Factory track's chain. The compact device shows the steps found so far, as a short list. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Source | Chooses the last minutes or a selected clip. |
| Fit | Sets Exact, Light or Broad. |
| Scope | Sets This project or All my work. |
| Name | Sets the skill's name. |

Remove a step that does not belong. Click the plate to show the device whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open the proposed skill full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole device shows every parameter, in two groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Example | Source, Steps |
| Skill | Name, Fit, Scope, Lands as |

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full device shows the proposed skill. It fills the pane with the skill's text and its steps, with the example beside them. The skill goes to Approvals as a proposal. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

In Clip view, a clip that you select can serve as the Source.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the proposal that waits, with Accept and Remove. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on Capture skill |
|---|---|
| Capture | Starts and ends the record of what you do by hand. |
| Record | Adds your spoken notes to the example. |
| Arm | Chooses the track whose agent will use the skill. |

#### To use Capture skill
1. Select the track whose agent should learn the task.
2. Press Capture. Do the task by hand.
3. Press Capture again. The device lists the steps it found.
4. Remove any step that does not belong. Choose a Fit.
5. Open Approvals. Read the proposal. Accept it or remove it.
6. Drag the accepted skill onto a Skillset rack.

#### Rules
- The skill arrives as a proposal. It is not admitted until a person has reviewed it.
- The device records only what you do while Capture is on.
- The example stays with the skill, so a reader can see where it came from.

#### Tips
- To teach a release routine, do one release by hand with Capture on.
- To make a safe skill, choose Exact for the first revision. Widen it later.

### 19.11 Runs

**Product:** Software Factory · **Kind:** instrument · **Pane:** a pane in Factory; also the Runs category in the browser

You need to see every attempt made on a piece of work, and which one stands. Runs lists the attempts and the task tree of each. A retry is a new attempt. It does not replace the old one.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Work | A piece of work | The selected session's work | The request whose attempts the device lists. |
| Attempt | A list of attempts | The latest | The attempt you read. |
| Show | All · Running · Stalled · Done | All | Filters the tasks in the tree. |
| Group by | Attempt · Agent · Day | Attempt | How the list is grouped. |
| View | Tree · Ruler | Tree | Tree: tasks as nested rows. Ruler: attempts along one ruler. |
| Compare | None, or a second attempt | None | Draws two attempts side by side. |
| Stands | An attempt | None | The attempt you accept. When you choose it, you review it. |

#### Views

```ui
device: software-factory:attempt-review
caption: Runs, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: in a pane of Factory, and in the Runs category of the browser. The folded Runs shows the work name and the number of attempts. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: in a pane of Factory. The compact Runs shows one bar for each attempt, coloured by state. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Attempt | Chooses the attempt you read. |
| Show | Filters the tasks to All, Running, Stalled or Done. |
| Group by | Groups the list by Attempt, Agent or Day. |
| Compare | Draws a second attempt beside the first. |

Click the plate to show Runs whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open the attempts full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole Runs shows every parameter, in three groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Work | Work, Attempt |
| Display | Show, Group by, View |
| Review | Compare, Stands |

Set Stands to the attempt you accept. The device does not choose for you.

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full Runs shows the attempts and the task tree of each, as a tree or along one ruler. Select a task to open its log in Clip view. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the latest attempt and its tasks, with a state light on each. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on Runs |
|---|---|
| Position | Shows the task, turn and step of the selected attempt. |
| Follow | Keeps the view on the needle of the running attempt. |
| Loop and punch | Sets the write window for the attempt you launch. |
| Play | Launches a new attempt of the selected task. |

#### To use Runs
1. Select a session. Open Runs from the browser.
2. Read the tree of the latest attempt.
3. Set Show to Stalled to find the task that stopped.
4. Set Compare to an earlier attempt. Read what changed.
5. Set Stands to the attempt you accept.

#### Rules
- An attempt is a record. A retry makes a new attempt.
- You choose the attempt that stands. The device does not choose for you.
- A running task has no drawn end, because nobody knows how long it takes.

#### Tips
- To find the first failure of a long run, set Show to Stalled and read the top row.
- To keep a good attempt, set Stands before you launch another.

### 19.12 Tasks

**Product:** Software Factory · **Kind:** instrument · **Track:** each Factory session; tasks are its clips

You need a list of what an agent is asked to do, with the state of each task. Tasks are the clips on a session's track. Each task shows how it stands, and Clip view shows its log.

#### How a task is drawn

| State | Drawn as |
|---|---|
| Done | Dim and settled. |
| Running | A blinking triangle and a fill that has no end. |
| Queued | A fast blink and no length. |
| Stopped | Ready, in full colour. |
| Stalled | A striped bar and an orange triangle, frozen where the output stopped. |

An empty square names what a click does: it stops the session after the current task. Double-click an empty square to write a task. Arm the track and press Record to speak one.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Name | Text | From the first message | The task's name. |
| State | Queued · Running · Stalled · Stopped · Done (reading) | Read from the session | Shows how the task stands. |
| Launch | Once · Hold · Loop | Once | Once: run one time. Hold: stay ready. Loop: repeat until the stop condition or the cap. |
| Then | Next task · Hand off to an agent · Stop | Next task | What follows the task. This is the follow action. |
| Launch quantize | Now · Next tool · Next turn | Next tool | When a launch or a steer lands. |
| Output style | Plain, warm or terse · Technical, narrative or list | Plain, technical | How the agent writes for this task. |
| Effort | Inherit · Low · Medium · High · Max | Inherit | Sets the effort for this task only. |
| Autonomy | Inherit · Suggest · Review · Auto | Inherit | Sets the autonomy for this task only. |

#### Views

```ui
device: software-factory:tasks
caption: Tasks, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: on a Factory session's track, as a clip. The folded Tasks shows the task name and its state. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: on a Factory session's track. The compact Tasks shows the task square with its progress. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Launch | Sets Once, Hold or Loop. |
| Then | Sets what follows the task: Next task, Hand off to an agent or Stop. |
| Effort | Sets the effort for this task only. |
| Autonomy | Sets the autonomy for this task only. |

Click the task to read its log in Clip view. Click the plate to show the task whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open the task log full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole Tasks shows every parameter, in three groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Task | Name, State |
| Launch | Launch, Then, Launch quantize |
| Style | Output style, Effort, Autonomy |

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full Tasks is the task log. It lays each tool that the agent used along the task's time, and marks the steers. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

In Clip view, the task is the clip. Its log shows there. Session view draws each task as a square in the column of its session.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the tasks of the selected session, with a state light on each. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on Tasks |
|---|---|
| Play | Launches the selected task, or every queued task. |
| Stop | Pauses at the next safe point. Press twice to cancel. |
| Record | Speaks a new task onto the armed track. |
| Loop and punch | Repeats the task, and sets the window in which the agent may write. |
| Keys and maps | Number keys launch rows. You can bind a word to a task. |

#### To use Tasks
1. Select a session. Double-click an empty square.
2. Write the task. Press Enter.
3. Set Then to Hand off if another agent should continue.
4. Press the number key of the row to launch the task.
5. Click the task. Read the log in Clip view.

#### Rules
- A row across sessions is a stop. A launch of a stop starts one task on each session.
- A loop ends at its stop condition or its cap.
- A stalled task keeps its place. Retry restarts it from its last good step.

#### Tips
- To run a batch, queue a task on each session and launch the row.
- To keep a long task from holding up others, set Launch quantize to Next turn.

### 19.13 Scope

**Product:** Software Factory · **Kind:** effect · **Chain:** after the agent, on any track an agent works on: fourth on a Factory session's chain; last on a Central workspace's chain

You need to limit the part of your world an agent may touch, and decide how
its writes arrive, wherever the agent works. Scope is the world device, the
same in every mode. It sets the reach from one object up to everything you
own, lists the places the agent may read and write inside it, and sets
whether a write arrives as a proposal, a draft or a direct change. The
Knowledge route sits inside it and decides what the agent may look up.

#### The rings

The picture shows nested rings. The smallest ring is one object: the open
page, item or symbol. The next rings are a scene, an Expression, and
everything in Central. The teal arc shows the reach for reads. The amber arc
shows the reach for writes. A place can be both. A basis readout shows the
revision the agent works from and the revision of its edits, for example r15
to r17.

#### Parameters

| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Reach | Object · Scene · Expression · Central | Object in Central; Scene in Factory and Expressions | The widest part of the world the agent may touch. A place outside it is closed, even when it is on a list. |
| Reads | A list of places, each with a light | The open object, the packet, and the scene's registers | The places the agent may read inside its Reach. A teal light marks a place in reach. |
| Writes | A list of places, each with a light | None in Central; the scene's layers in Factory | The places the agent may write. A place not on the list is read-only. An amber light marks a place in reach. |
| Write mode | Propose · Draft · Direct | Propose | How a write arrives. Propose: as a proposal that waits. Draft: in a draft that you review. Direct: in the thing itself, with a receipt. With Writes empty, the agent writes nowhere. |
| Knowledge | See Knowledge route | As Knowledge route | The knowledge sources and bounds. |
| Basis | A revision pair (reading) | Read from the world | The revision the agent started from and the revision of its edits. |
| Receipts | A list (reading) | Read from the agent | One receipt for each write: the agent, the time, the place and the change. |

#### Views

```ui
device: software-factory:context-write-mode
caption: Scope, on a fixture of readings.
```

| View | Holds |
|---|---|
| Folded | Scope and the Reach. |
| Compact | The rings, teal over the top for reads and amber under the bottom for writes; the basis. Macros: Reach, Write mode, and the counts of read and write places. |
| Whole | Band one: the rings with the reach handle, Reach with Confirm and Cancel, Write mode, the receipts. Band two: Reads, Writes, Knowledge and the basis. |
| Full | The rings at pane size: the world the agent may touch, teal where it reads and amber where it writes, with each receipt numbered on the place it wrote. |
| Widget | Small rings, the Reach, the Write mode and the last receipts. Press a receipt to read it whole. |

#### Transport

| Control | Effect on Scope |
|---|---|
| Arm | Direct writes land only when Arm is on. With Arm off, Direct acts as Propose. |
| Loop and punch | Sets the window in which writes may land. |
| Re-enable | Lights when you change something the agent wrote. Press it to hand it back. |
| Draw | Marks a place or a tile as a reference for the agent. |
| Key, root and scale | Shows what the agent will read: its size and its reach. |

#### To use Scope

1. Select the track of an agent and open Device view.
2. Turn Reach to the smallest part of the world the work needs.
3. Open Scope whole. Check the lists of Reads and Writes. Add a place to
   Writes only if the agent must write there.
4. Choose a Write mode. Start with Propose.
5. After you trust the result, choose Draft or Direct, and arm the track.
6. Read the receipts here, or in the Ledger.

#### Rules

- Scope limits the agent. A place outside the Reach is neither readable nor
  writable.
- The agent cannot widen its own Reach or its own lists. Only you change them.
- A place that you raise into wider reach asks you first.
- A place in the Reach is available to the agent. It is not loaded into every
  prompt.
- Every write carries a receipt.
- Direct needs Arm on.

#### Tips

- To keep an agent to one file, set Reach to Object and leave Writes empty.
- To let an agent read widely but write little, raise Reach and keep Writes
  short.
- To try a risky change, set Write mode to Draft and review the draft.

### 19.14 Ledger

**Product:** Software Factory · **Kind:** instrument · **Track:** Factory's Main track

You need a record of what the agents did and what came of it. The Ledger lists every task with its receipt. It shows what landed, what was approved and what drew a comment.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Period | This run · Today · All | Today | The span of tasks in the list. |
| Agent | All, or one agent | All | Shows the tasks of one agent. |
| Outcome | All · Landed · Approved · Commented · Refused | All | Shows tasks with one outcome. |
| Order | Newest first · By agent · By task | Newest first | The order of the list. |
| Receipt | Open · Closed | Closed | Opens the receipt of the selected task. |

#### What a receipt holds

A receipt holds the number of the task, its name, the agent, the outcome, the revision it started from and who accepted it. It also lists the files that changed. A receipt is made when the task writes. The Ledger does not edit a receipt.

#### Views

```ui
device: software-factory:ledger
caption: Ledger, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: on the Main track's chain. The folded Ledger shows the title "Ledger" and the count of tasks today. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: on the Main track's chain. The compact Ledger shows one line for each task: the agent's mark, the task and its state. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Period | Sets This run, Today or All. |
| Agent | Shows the tasks of all agents or of one agent. |
| Outcome | Shows tasks with one outcome. |
| Order | Sets Newest first, By agent or By task. |

Click a task to open its log in Clip view. Click the plate to show the Ledger whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open it full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole Ledger shows every parameter, in two groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Filter | Period, Agent, Outcome, Order |
| Receipt | Receipt |

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full Ledger shows the list of tasks, with each receipt open beside its task. A receipt holds the task number, the agent, the outcome, the start revision, who accepted it, and the files that changed. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the last receipts. Each line is a task and its receipt, for example "#212 re-plan port · landed". Select a receipt to read it in full. Click the task to open its log. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on the Ledger |
|---|---|
| Position | Shows the task of the receipt you selected. |
| Follow | Keeps the list on the newest receipt. |
| Capture | Keeps a receipt as an example. |

#### To use the Ledger
1. Select the Main track. Open Device view.
2. Read the Ledger card. Each line is a task.
3. Set Outcome to Commented to find tasks that need an answer.
4. Select a task. Open its receipt.
5. Click the task to open its log in Clip view.

#### Rules
- Every write has a receipt, and every receipt is in the Ledger.
- The Ledger only reads. It changes no receipt.
- The list follows the sessions of the Main track, so a new session appears on its own.

#### Tips
- To prepare the Day's face 2, read today's Ledger first.
- To audit one agent, set Agent to that agent and Period to All.

### 19.15 Approvals

**Product:** Software Factory · **Kind:** effect · **Track:** Factory's Main track

You need one place to answer what the agents ask. Approvals holds every proposal and every request that waits for you. Nothing in it lands until you answer.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Queue | A count (reading) | Read from the agents | The number of proposals and requests that wait. |
| Show | Proposals · Requests · Both | Both | Proposals hold changes. Requests ask for a decision, for example a deploy. |
| Default answer | Ask · Session · Always | Ask | The default answer to a request. The Gateway holds the same setting for each session. |
| Wait | 15 min–24 h | 4 h | How long a request waits before it counts as unanswered. |
| On no answer | Hold · Deny | Hold | What happens when nobody answers. |
| Order | Oldest first · By agent · By risk | Oldest first | The order of the queue. |

#### Views

```ui
device: software-factory:approvals
caption: Approvals, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: on the Main track's chain. The folded Approvals shows the title "Approvals" and the count of items that wait. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: on the Main track's chain. The compact Approvals shows one line for each item: the agent's mark, the item and a button. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Show | Chooses Proposals, Requests or Both. |
| Default answer | Sets Ask, Session or Always. |
| Wait | Sets how long a request waits before it counts as unanswered. |
| On no answer | Sets Hold or Deny. |

Click Open to read an item. Click Approve to accept it. Click the plate to show Approvals whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open the queue full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole Approvals shows every parameter, in two groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Queue | Queue, Show, Order |
| Defaults | Default answer, Wait, On no answer |

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full Approvals shows the queue. Beside it, the diff of the selected proposal shows. The queue shows the author of each item. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the proposal cards. Each card names the agent and the change. Each card has Approve, Changes and Open. Click Open to read the diff. Click Approve to accept the proposal. Click Changes to ask for a different result. Nothing lands until you answer. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on Approvals |
|---|---|
| Arm | With Arm on, writes inside the Scope land directly. They do not enter the queue. |
| Re-enable | Gives back what the agent held. |
| Metronome | Shows the beat at which waiting requests count their time. |

#### To use Approvals
1. Select the Main track. Open Device view. A number on the title shows the queue.
2. Read the first item. Click Open to read its diff.
3. Click Approve to accept it. Click Changes to ask for a different result.
4. For a request, read the question. Choose Approve or Deny.
5. Empty the queue before you end the day.

#### Rules
- Deny is always yours. Only the On no answer setting can deny for you, and you set it.
- An approved proposal lands with a receipt.
- A request that is not answered stays in the queue. It does not land.
- The queue shows the author of each item.

#### Tips
- To clear many proposals fast, set Order to By risk and read the risky ones first.
- To stop a deploy from leaving at night, leave On no answer at Hold.

### 19.16 Run

**Product:** Software Factory · **Kind:** instrument · **Track:** one lane for each session in Arrangement view

You need to see the course of one session along the clock. A Run is one session's stream of tasks, drawn on a ruler. A needle shows where the session has got to.

#### The lanes

A lane has task blocks. A done block is solid. A running block fades out at the right and has no end. A stalled block is striped, and the needle stops where the output stopped. A queued block is dashed and has no length. Under each lane, the event lanes show Messages, Files, Terminal, Web and Graph. Click an event to open it in the matching widget. A line marks the present moment.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Session | A session | The selected session | The session the lane shows. |
| Watch | In · Auto · Off | Auto | In: always stream this session into the widgets. Auto: stream it when it is armed or selected. Off: do not stream it. |
| Telemetry | On · Off | On | On: the session feeds the widgets, the Ledger and other agents. Off: it works in silence. |
| Solo | On · Off | Off | Only soloed sessions may run. |
| Event lanes | Messages · Files · Terminal · Web · Graph | Messages | The event lanes under the thread. |
| Overview | On · Off | On | Shows the strip above the ruler. |
| Needle | A time (reading) | Read from the session | The time of the last check-in. |
| Takes | A list of attempts | The latest | The attempts under the lane. |

#### Views

```ui
device: software-factory:run-device
caption: Run, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: as one lane of the Run track in Arrangement view, and on the session's chain. The folded Run shows the session name and the needle time. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: on the session's chain. The compact Run shows the lane, with a needle and a state light. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Watch | Sets In, Auto or Off for the stream into the widgets. |
| Telemetry | Turns on or off the feed to the widgets, the Ledger and other agents. |
| Solo | Lets only soloed sessions run. |
| Event lanes | Chooses the lanes under the thread. |

Click the plate to show the Run whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open the thread full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole Run shows every parameter, in three groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Feed | Session, Watch, Telemetry, Solo |
| Lanes | Event lanes, Overview, Needle |
| Takes | Takes |

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full Run is the thread. It shows tasks, tool rows, steer lines, proposal cards and the stall card. The needle shows the last check-in. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

In Clip view, the lane holds the clips of the session. A new attempt is a take under the same lane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the session's state, its needle time and its task now. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on the Run |
|---|---|
| Record | Lays what the session did into Arrangement view as clips, with takes under them. |
| Follow | Scrolls the ruler with the needles. |
| Position | Shows the task, turn and step of the needle. |
| Metronome | Sets how often the needle moves. |
| Arm | Records your moves of the agent's settings as lanes under the track. |

#### To use the Run
1. Press Tab to open Arrangement view.
2. Find the lane of the session. Read its needle.
3. If the needle has stopped, read the stall readout. It names how long the lane has stalled.
4. Expand the lane. Click an event to open it in its widget.
5. Press Retry on the track to restart a stalled task from its last good step.

#### Rules
- The needle shows the last check-in. It does not predict the end.
- A stalled needle freezes where the output stopped.
- A new attempt is a take under the same lane. The earlier take stays.

#### Tips
- To watch only the sessions that matter, set Watch to Off on the others.
- To keep a loud session out of the Ledger, set its Telemetry to Off.

### 19.17 Agentset rack

**Product:** Software Factory · **Kind:** rack · **Chain:** a group's chain in Factory

You need a team of agents to work as one unit. The Agentset rack holds one chain for each member. Members nest as they were spawned.

#### The members

Each chain is one agent. The lead is at the top. Its helpers sit below it, with one step of indent for each level. A chip on a chain names the member's model. A stalled member has an outline in the stall colour. A group header folds the crew. When you fold it, its slots still show the tasks of the children. A launch of the group fires them all.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Members | A list of agents | The lead | The chains of the rack. Drag an agent from the browser to add one. |
| Selected member | One member | The lead | The chain whose device shows in the pool. |
| Fan-out | A number · No limit | 2 | The most helpers that run at once. The default is a start value, not a limit. |
| Depth | A number · No limit | 2 | How deep helpers may spawn their own helpers. The default is a start value, not a limit. |
| Hand-back | Raw · Digest · Summary | Summary | What a helper returns to its parent. |
| Subagent model | A model | The lead's model | The default model for everything the group spawns. A member can set its own. |
| Member state | Working · Idle · Stalled · Done (reading) | Read from each session | The state of each chain. |

#### Views

```ui
device: software-factory:agentset-rack
caption: Agentset rack, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: on a group's chain in Factory. The folded rack shows the crew name and the count of members. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: on a group's chain. The compact rack shows the member chains, nested as they were spawned. A chip on each chain names the member's model. A stalled member has an orange outline. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Fan-out | Sets the most helpers that run at once. |
| Depth | Sets how deep helpers may spawn their own helpers. |
| Hand-back | Sets Raw, Digest or Summary for what a helper returns. |
| Subagent model | Sets the default model for everything the group spawns. |

With many members, the chains group by state, and each group shows its count. Click a member to open its thread. Click the plate to show the rack whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open the crew full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole rack shows every parameter, in three groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Members | Members, Selected member, Member state |
| Limits | Fan-out, Depth |
| Return | Hand-back, Subagent model |

Drag an agent from the browser onto the rack to add a member. The rack sets no upper limit on members.

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full rack shows the crew as a tree. Each member shows its thread and the files it touched. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the crew tree with a light on each member. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on the Agentset rack |
|---|---|
| Play | Launches every queued task of the crew. |
| Stop | Pauses every member at its next safe point. Press twice to cancel. |
| Tap | Asks every member for a status line. |
| Quantize | Sets when Stop and Steer land for the crew. |
| Keys and maps | Number keys launch rows across the members. |

#### To use the Agentset rack
1. Drag an agentset from the browser onto the track of the lead.
2. Select the group header. Open Device view.
3. Set Fan-out to the number of helpers that may run together.
4. Set Depth to 1 if helpers must not spawn others.
5. Choose Hand-back. Choose Summary to keep the lead's context short.
6. Click a member to read its thread.

#### Rules
- The rack sets no upper limit on members, Fan-out or Depth. A limit is a value you set.
- Each member keeps its own chain. A helper shares the lead's gateway and git seat.
- A stalled member does not stop the others. The group lights Retry.
- Hard stop on the group stops every session in it. The work written so far stays as a draft.

#### Tips
- To keep a lead's context small, set Hand-back to Summary.
- To save cost, set Subagent model to a smaller model for the group.
- To find the slow member, look for the orange outline.

### 19.18 Git / landing

**Product:** Workcell · **Kind:** effect · **Chain:** last device on a Factory session's chain

You need the agent's work to land safely in the project, with checks you can read. Git / landing is the place where the work ends. It commits the work, pushes it and lands it as a merge request.

#### The branch diagram

The line at the bottom is main. The branch leaves it and runs to the right. Each dot is a commit. A pulsing hollow dot at the end is uncommitted work. Text above the branch shows lines added and removed, and the number of files. Text below shows how far the branch is ahead of main.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Branch | A name (reading) | The seat's branch | The branch the agent works on. |
| Seat | A seat (reading) | The seat of the session | The checkout where the agent works. See Seat. |
| Commit | Per turn · Per task · Manual | Per task | When the agent commits. |
| Push | Off · On land | Off | Off: the work stays on this machine. On land: the branch goes to the remote when you land it. |
| Land as | Draft merge request · Merge request · Merge | Draft merge request | How the work lands. |
| Checks | A count (reading) | Read from the remote | The checks that passed, of all the checks, and those that still run. |
| Changes | Lines and files (reading) | Read from the seat | Lines added and removed, and files changed. |
| Uncommitted | A count (reading) | Read from the seat | The files that have no commit. |
| Land | A button | — | Lands the branch as set in Land as. |

#### Views

```ui
device: workcell:git-seat
caption: Git / landing, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: on a Factory session's chain, as the last device. The folded device shows the title "Git" and the branch name. A child session shows "shared with lead". Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: on a Factory session's chain. The compact device shows the branch diagram. The line at the bottom is main. Each dot is a commit. A pulsing hollow dot is uncommitted work. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Commit | Sets Per turn, Per task or Manual. |
| Push | Sets Off or On land. |
| Land as | Sets Draft merge request, Merge request or Merge. |
| Land | Lands the branch as set in Land as. |

Click the plate to show the device whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open the branch full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole device shows every parameter, in three groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Seat | Branch, Seat, Uncommitted |
| Cadence | Commit, Push |
| Landing | Land as, Checks, Changes, Land |

The checks come from the remote. The device does not make them up.

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full device shows the branch. It lists the commits, the files and the checks, with the diff beside them. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the branch, the changes, the checks and the Land button. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on Git / landing |
|---|---|
| Play | With Land as set, lands the work when the last task ends and the checks pass. |
| Loop and punch | Sets the step before which nothing is committed, and the step after which nothing is. |
| Arm | With Arm off, the commits wait as proposals for your approval. |
| Capture | Keeps the diff as an example. |

#### To use Git / landing
1. Select a session. Open Device view. Read the Git card.
2. Choose Commit. Use Per task for work you review task by task.
3. Leave Push on Off while you test.
4. When the work is ready, choose Land as. Press Land.
5. Read the checks. Open the merge request when they pass.

#### Rules
- The agent commits only from a seat. The device does not create a new checkout.
- Land does not merge by itself unless Land as is Merge.
- A child session shares the lead's seat and branch.
- The checks come from the remote. The device does not make them up.
- After the work lands, the seat returns to the main branch.

#### Tips
- To keep the history small, set Commit to Per task.
- To test a change before anyone sees it, keep Push on Off and read the diff.
- To get a second view, land as a draft merge request and ask a reviewer.

### 19.19 Seat

**Product:** Workcell · **Kind:** effect · **Chain:** inside the Git / landing device; whole in the pool

You need a place where an agent can change a project without touching other work. A seat is a checkout of a project that agents share. The Seat device claims one for a session, and releases it when the work has landed.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Product | A project | The session's project | The project that the seat holds. |
| Seat | The seats of this machine | The free seat | The seat the session joins. |
| Lane | A branch name | A new branch from main | The line of work that the seat holds. |
| Claim | Free · Yours · Another agent's (reading) | Read from the register | Who holds the seat. |
| Join | Attach to the lane · Start a new lane | Attach to the lane | Attach: work beside the agents on the standing lane. New lane: replace the standing lane. |
| Co-workers | A list (reading) | Read from the claim | The agents that share this lane. |
| Uncommitted | A count (reading) | Read from the seat | The files that have no commit. |
| Release | A button | — | Releases the seat after the work has landed. |

#### Views

```ui
device: workcell:seat
caption: Seat, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: inside the Git / landing device. The folded Seat shows the title "Seat" and the claim. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: inside the Git / landing device. The compact Seat shows the seat name, the lane and the co-workers. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Seat | Chooses the seat the session joins. |
| Join | Sets Attach to the lane or Start a new lane. |
| Lane | Sets the branch name of the line of work. |
| Release | Releases the seat after the work has landed. |

Click the Git title to show the Seat whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open the seats full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole Seat shows every parameter, in two groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Claim | Product, Seat, Claim, Co-workers |
| Work | Lane, Join, Uncommitted, Release |

A new lane does not replace uncommitted work.

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full Seat shows the seats of the machine. Each seat shows its lane, its claim and its uncommitted work. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the seat name, the lane and the claim. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on the Seat |
|---|---|
| Link | Shows the seats of your other machines. |
| Play | Joins the seat when the first task starts. |
| Stop | Keeps the claim. Release gives the seat up. |

#### To use the Seat
1. Select a session. Open the Git card whole in the pool.
2. Read Claim. If another agent holds the seat, choose Attach to join as a co-worker.
3. To start different work on the same project, choose Start a new lane.
4. Work. Land the branch.
5. Press Release. The seat returns to the main branch and the claim clears.

#### Rules
- A seat holds one lane at a time. Several agents can work in one lane.
- The device does not create a new checkout. If every seat is busy, attach to a lane or take one over.
- A new lane does not replace uncommitted work.
- A commit from a place that is not a seat is refused.
- A seat left on a landed branch is not finished work.

#### Tips
- To work next to another agent, choose Attach.
- To clear a seat after a merge, press Release.

### 19.20 Remote carrier

**Product:** Workcell · **Kind:** effect · **Chain:** the fourth chain of the Gateways rack

You need to choose where a session runs. The Remote carrier moves a session from this machine to another machine of yours. It shows whether that machine can carry the session now.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Where | This machine · A connected machine | This machine | The place where the session runs. |
| Machine | The machines you connected | None | The connected machine that carries the session. |
| Standing | Ready · Composed · Refused (reading) | Read from the machine | Whether the machine can carry the session now. A refused machine names the reason. |
| Grant | The operations allowed (reading) | Read from the machine | What this machine may do for you. |
| Credential | Valid · Expired · Revoked (reading) | Read from the machine | Whether the machine accepts you. An expired credential is not the same as a revoked one. |
| Round trip | Milliseconds (reading) | Read from the link | The time for a message to go and return. |

#### Views

```ui
device: workcell:remote-carrier
caption: Remote carrier, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: as the fourth chain of the Gateways rack. The folded carrier shows the title "Remote carrier" and its state dot. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: as the fourth chain of the Gateways rack. The compact carrier shows the machines, each with a dot. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Where | Chooses This machine or a connected machine. |
| Machine | Chooses the connected machine that carries the session. |
| Standing | Shows Ready, Composed or Refused. |
| Round trip | Shows the time for a message to go and return. |

Click the row to read its Standing. Click the plate to show the carrier whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open the machine full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole carrier shows every parameter, in two groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Place | Where, Machine |
| Readings | Standing, Grant, Credential, Round trip |

A refused carrier shows its reason. An expired credential and a revoked credential show as different states.

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full carrier shows one machine. The pane shows its readiness, its sessions and its grant. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the machine name, its dot and the round trip. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on the Remote carrier |
|---|---|
| Link | Adds the machines that share your ground. |
| Rate | Shows the round trip of the link. |

#### To use the Remote carrier
1. Select the Main track. Open Device view.
2. Click the Remote carrier row. Read its Standing.
3. Choose Where. Choose a machine.
4. Open the Gateway of the session. Set Transport to Remote.
5. Press Play. The session runs on the other machine.

#### Rules
- A refused or composed carrier does not show as live.
- The carrier does not create a machine. It uses a machine you connected.
- A session on another machine keeps its own seat and its own receipts.
- Secrets stay on the machine that holds them. The carrier sends a reference.

#### Tips
- To run a long job away from your laptop, choose a connected machine and set Transport to Remote.
- To find out why a carrier is refused, read its reason in the Standing row.

### 19.21 Link

**Product:** Workcell · **Kind:** effect · **Track:** Factory's Main track

You need your sessions on several machines to keep one ledger and one clock. Link joins them. Agents on other machines and in other harnesses then show beside your own.

#### Parameters
| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Link | On · Off | Off | Joins this machine to your others. |
| Share | Ledger · Clock · Both | Both | What the machines keep in common. |
| Machines | The machines you connected | All | The machines in the link. |
| Peers | A count (reading) | Read from the link | The agents and machines that joined. |
| Lag | Milliseconds (reading) | Read from the link | The time for a change to reach the others. |

#### Views

```ui
device: workcell:link
caption: Link, on a fixture of readings.
```

##### Folded

Where: on the Main track's chain. The folded Link shows the title "Link" and the number of peers. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: on the Main track's chain. The compact Link shows the machines as dots joined by lines. It has four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Link | Turns the link on or off. |
| Share | Chooses Ledger, Clock or Both. |
| Machines | Chooses the machines in the link. |
| Lag | Shows the time for a change to reach the others. |

Click the plate to show the Link whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open the machines full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole Link shows every parameter, in two groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Link | Link, Share, Machines |
| Readings | Peers, Lag |

A machine that is not reachable shows as such. It stays in the list.

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full Link shows the machines. Each machine shows its sessions and its seats. Select a session of another machine to read its thread. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the peers and the link state. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport
| Control | Effect on the Link |
|---|---|
| Link | Turns the link on or off. |
| Tap | Asks the agents on all machines for a status line. |
| Rate | Shows the lag of the link. |

#### To use the Link
1. Press Link in the transport.
2. Open Device view on the Main track. Read the Peers.
3. Choose what to Share.
4. Select a session of another machine. Read its thread.

#### Rules
- Linked machines keep one ledger. A receipt made on one machine shows on all.
- A machine that is not reachable is shown as such. It does not leave the list.
- Link shares the ledger and the clock. It does not share a seat.

#### Tips
- To follow a run on a far machine, turn Link on and select its session.
- To keep a machine private for a while, remove it from Machines.

## 20. Expressions

Expressions is where the DLW makes things you can see and hear. Particles and symbols move on a plate, and the plate sounds.

### 20.1 Field

**Product:** Expressions · **Kind:** instrument · **Track:** An Expression

The Field is the medium that you see and hear. Use it to give an idea a form that moves and sounds, so that you can stay with it and listen.

#### What the field holds

The field holds three things in one state. Sound and picture both come from this one state. When one changes, the other changes.

| Part | What it is |
|---|---|
| Particles | Many small marks. They gather into the shapes you give them and move by the forces you set. |
| Symbols | Letters, shapes and images that hold a place, drift, pull on each other or push apart. A symbol can have a voice. |
| Plate | The surface under the field. In three dimensions it is a chamber. The pattern on the plate is the pattern you hear. |

The pitch rides the lines where the plate stands still. A symbol with a voice sounds at its own frequency, or follows the plate.

#### Parameters

| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sound | On · Off | On | Lets the field sound. The picture stays when sound is off. |
| Level | −60 to 0 dB | −12 dB | Sets the loudness of the whole field. |
| Medium | Plate · Chamber | Plate | Chooses a flat plate or a three-dimensional chamber. |
| Tempo | 20 to 240 | 96 | Sets how fast the field forms and breathes. |
| Hold to change | A ratio, such as 5:3 | 5:3 | Sets how long a scene holds a figure compared with how long it changes. |
| Key source | Manual · Sky | Manual | Manual uses the resonance you set. Sky takes the resonance from the sky over your world. |
| Resonance | 20 to 4000 Hz | 396 Hz | The frequency that the field is tuned to. |
| Symbol voices | On · Off | On | Lets symbols that have a voice sound. |
| Pulse | On · Off | Off | Pulses the field on the beat. |
| Lights | Dim · Full | Full | Turns the lights down so that one symbol stands alone. |

#### Views

```ui
device: expressions:field
caption: Field, on a fixture of readings.
```

In Clip view, the Field plays the scene clip that you select. The clip sets how long the scene holds and how it changes to the next.

##### Folded

Where: on the chain of an Expression, as its first device. The folded Field shows its name and the sound light. The light is on only when the field sounds. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: on the chain of an Expression, as its first device. The compact Field shows a small live picture of the plate and the particles, and four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Level | Sets the loudness of the whole field. |
| Tempo | Sets how fast the field forms and breathes. |
| Resonance | Sets the frequency that the field is tuned to. |
| Hold to change | Sets how long a scene holds a figure compared with how long it changes. |

Click the plate to show the Field whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open it full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole Field shows every parameter, in three groups.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Sound | Sound, Level, Medium, Key source, Resonance |
| Time | Tempo, Hold to change, Pulse |
| Voices | Symbol voices, Lights |

A change you make in the pool applies at once. It does not add the Field to a chain.

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full Field is the living field stage. Particles gather into the shapes of the symbols. The plate shows the pattern that you hear. Press Play in the transport. Move the pointer through the field to stir the particles. Set Lights to Dim in Device view to let one symbol stand alone. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows a small copy of the field and a level meter. The meter shows the level of the whole field. Click the copy to open the Field in the pane that has focus. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport

| Control | Effect on the Field |
|---|---|
| Play | Plays the scenes of the Expression. |
| Stop | Stops the field and rewinds the scene. |
| Record | Records a take of the field. |
| Overdub | Lays a new take over the scene. |
| Arm | Records the Studio as a take. Every control that you move becomes an automation lane. |
| Tempo | Sets the speed of the field. |
| Time signature | Sets the ratio of hold to change. |
| Metronome | Pulses the field. |
| Key, root and scale | Tunes the resonance of the field. |
| Loop | Loops the scenes. |
| Capture | Keeps your last gestures as a new scene. |

#### To use the Field

1. Open an Expression. The Field fills the pane.
2. Press Play. The particles gather and the plate sounds.
3. Open the Studio in Device view. Add a formation, or select a symbol.
4. Sweep the Resonance parameter. Watch the pattern form on the plate.
5. Give a symbol a voice in the Sound device. Listen to it in the field.
6. Press Capture to keep the figure as a scene. Save the Expression.

#### Rules

- Sound and picture come from one state. A device cannot change one without the other.
- A parameter that you hold stays live when its widget is not on the rack.
- The Field does not judge the scene. It answers when you play it.
- A camera move does not change the sound.

#### Tips

- To hear a figure before you keep it, press Play and sweep Resonance slowly.
- To keep the field quiet while you build, set Sound to Off.
- To study one symbol, set Lights to Dim and select the symbol.
- To follow the planets, set Key source to Sky.

### 20.2 Studio

**Product:** Expressions · **Kind:** rack · **Track:** An Expression. It fills Device view in Expressions.

The Studio holds the controls that shape the field. Use it to pick a tool, act on the symbol you selected, and shape the form, the material and the motion.

#### The sections

The Studio has eight sections. Each section is one chain of controls. Select a section to open it in the pane.

| Section | What it holds |
|---|---|
| Tools | Select, Orbit, Form, Bind and Capture. The Library opens from here. |
| Selected | The actions for the symbol you selected: Bind, Follow, Exact, Duplicate and Source. |
| Formation | How the particles gather: how many points, how many folds, and the shape. |
| Material | The palette, the glow and the grain. |
| Motion | The speed, the turbulence and how symbols follow. |
| Body | Which layers join the field: the torus, the sky and the clock. The graph and Epii also switch here. |
| Nara | Your personal layer. It stays yours however the scene is shared. |
| Capture | Keeps the field as a still or a video. |

Behind the sections stand the devices that govern the field. Each is a whole device with every parameter it needs.

#### Parameters

| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tool | Select · Orbit · Form · Bind · Capture | Select | The tool that the pointer uses. |
| Selected action | Bind · Follow · Exact · Duplicate · Source | None | Acts on the selected symbol. Source opens where the symbol came from. |
| Density | 60 to 220 points | 160 | How many points make the formation. |
| Symmetry | 3 to 9 folds | 5 | How many times the formation repeats around its centre. |
| Shape | Ring · Spiral · Lattice | Ring | The form that points gather into. |
| Glow and grain | Glow 0 to 100% · Grain 0 to 100% | 50% · 30% | How bright the marks shine, and how coarse they look. |
| Speed | 0 to 100% | 50% | How fast the field moves. |
| Turbulence | 0 to 100% | 20% | How much the field stirs. |
| Follow | Free · Bind | Bind | Free symbols move alone. Bind makes them follow their binding. |
| Body | Torus, Sky, Clock, Graph, Epii: On · Off | Torus, Sky and Clock On | Adds or removes each layer. |
| Nara | On · Off, then Private · Circle | On, Private | Shows your personal layer. Private keeps it to you. |
| Capture | Still · Video | Still | The kind of take that Capture makes. |

#### Views

```ui
device: expressions:studio
caption: Studio, on a fixture of readings.
```

In Clip view, the Capture section keeps your last gestures as a new scene clip.

##### Folded

Where: in Device view, in Expressions. The folded Studio shows its name, the open section and the save state: Draft, Saved, or Edited since save. Click the title to unfold it.

##### Compact

Where: in Device view, in Expressions. The compact Studio shows a strip of the eight section names, with the open one marked, and four macros.

| Macro | What it does |
|---|---|
| Density | Sets how many points make the formation. |
| Glow | Sets how bright the marks shine. |
| Speed | Sets how fast the field moves. |
| Turbulence | Sets how much the field stirs. |

Click the plate to show the Studio whole in the pool. Click ⤢ to open it full in a pane.

##### Whole

Where: the pool under the browser. The whole Studio shows every parameter, in eight groups, one for each section.

| Group | Parameters |
|---|---|
| Tools | Tool |
| Selected | Selected action |
| Formation | Density, Symmetry, Shape |
| Material | Glow and grain |
| Motion | Speed, Turbulence, Follow |
| Body | Body |
| Nara | Nara |
| Capture | Capture |

A change you make in the pool applies at once. It does not add the Studio to a chain.

##### Full

Where: a pane. The full Studio shows the open section at full size, as a surface to work on. Formation shows the form, Material the palette, Motion the flow, and Body the layers of the field. Select a section name to open that section in the pane. The controls stay in Device view, below the pane.

##### Widget

Where: the rail on the right. The widget shows the tool that you hold and the selected symbol. Click the symbol to open the Selected section in Device view. The widget stays when you change mode.

#### Transport

| Control | Effect on the Studio |
|---|---|
| Arm | Records every Studio move as a take. |
| Re-enable | Gives back the values that you hold. |
| Capture | Keeps your last gestures as a scene. |
| Draw | Draws a form into the field with the Form tool. |
| Keys and maps | Maps keys and gestures to Studio controls. |

#### To use the Studio

1. Open an Expression and look at Device view.
2. Select the Form tool. Draw a form in the field.
3. Open Formation. Set Density and Symmetry until the form reads.
4. Select a symbol. Press Bind to tie it to a source.
5. Open Material and Motion. Set Glow, Speed and Turbulence.
6. Press Capture, then press Save.

#### Rules

- The Studio controls stay out of the field pane. The field pane shows only the field.
- Your personal layer does not become portable because it is drawn. Private keeps it to you.
- The save state shows Draft, Saved and Edited since save as three separate states.
- A control that you hold shows the hand-back light. Press the light to give control back.

#### Tips

- To work on one symbol, select it and open the Selected section.
- To share a scene without your personal layer, set Nara to Private before you save.
- To try a form fast, draw it with the Form tool, then press Capture.
- To open a device at full size, select its section name.

### 20.3 Physics

**Product:** Expressions · **Kind:** effect · **Chain:** An Expression's chain, after the Field

The Physics device sets how the particles move: how they swirl, slow down, fall and return to their shapes. Use it to make the field thick, light, calm or wild.

#### Parameters

| Parameter | Values | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Viscosity | 0.2 to 1.01 | 0.94 | The share of speed that a particle keeps each step. A high value lets a gesture linger. |
| Vortex force | −25 to 25 | 1.4 | The strength of the swirl. A negative value turns the swirl the other way. |
| Vortex radius | 20 to 2000 pixels | 450 | The reach of each swirl. |
| Return spring | −5 to 25 | 1.1 | The pull that brings a particle back to its shape. |
